⏺ Exitplanmode(plan: |
  # Implement `knowledge-commons-process-pipeline.md` (spec v1.2)
  
  ## Context
  
  The generated `/process` pipeline is missing two of the four stages of the model it was built
  from. Nothing in the template **preserves** the raw material (step 3 searches for a source note,
  step 9 stamps one, nothing writes one), and nothing **synthesizes** — the synthesis role was
  specified into four artifacts and implemented in none. The absence has already cost this graph:
  its founding source note points at a pruned transcript in a gitignored directory.
  
  Because the pipeline could not produce a synthesis, `claude-code-plugins` put its synthesis on
  the input side — the chronicle is registered as a second *source* tier — and grew containment
  machinery around that inversion (a primary/fallback ordering, an overlapping-tiers guard, a
  transcript-survival `grep`, a decision note enshrining it).
  
  This implements the spec's correction: preserve and synthesize become named stages, extraction
  reads the raw material, the synthesis becomes a sibling output rather than an extraction
  intermediate, and this project migrates onto the corrected model. Ships as knowledge-commons
  0.6.0.
  
  ## Scope
  
  **In:** §6 steps 1–5, plus §7.1, §7.2, §7.4.
  
  **Out:** §6 step 6 / §7.3 — the end-to-end `/process` run on the authoring session
  `f64160a1-e82b-4015-bd19-08331d49a995`. Derek has run that separately. Consequently §5.3's
  `observations/…` row (new observations "written by the `/process` run over it") is also out; the
  spec says those are not hand-authored.
  
  Branching from `main` (77781bc). The remote branch `worktree-knowledge-commons-v06-executable`
  is deliberately ignored per Derek.
  
  ## Things the spec gets wrong or is silent on
  
  Surfaced rather than papered over. None is blocking; the first three change what I do.
  
  1. **Five vs six deltas.** §5.5 enumerates **six** and explains why (`synthesis-stage` was split
     into `synthesis-in-plan` + `synthesis-write` at v1.1 because its substance straddles the
     approval gate). §5.1, §5.2, §6 step 4 and §7.4 all still say "five" — v1.1 updated the table
     and not its four references. Going with six.
  2. **`/graph-patch` cannot see the new deltas.** It reads
     `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/references/deltas.md`, which resolves to the *installed cache* at
     `~/.claude/plugins/cache/ddehart-plugins/knowledge-commons/0.5.0/` — not this working tree.
     New deltas land in the working copy and would be invisible. The spec is silent. Resolution:
     invoke `/graph-patch` and direct it to `plugins/knowledge-commons/references/deltas.md`,
     the authoritative current log for the repo that *is* the plugin's source. Not syncing the
     global cache — three other projects install from it.
  3. **`.claude/session-exports/` is not empty** (§7.2 asserts it is). It holds a 99 KB export of
     the authoring session made today. I'll copy that to scratch for the false-positive test
     rather than re-exporting, which is what §7.2 wants the test to have.
  4. **Parent spec drift, not fixed.** `docs/specs/knowledge-commons.md` §5's `.commons.yml`
     sketch still comments synthesis as "intermediate for rich bounded sources" — the framing D3
     corrects. §5.1 does not list the parent spec, so I'm leaving it and flagging it here.
  
  ## Phase 1 — Plugin changes (§5.1, §6 step 1)
  
  Eight files under `plugins/knowledge-commons/`, plus one new script.
  
  | File | Change |
  |---|---|
  | `references/templates/process.md` | Renumber to the §5.4 sequence (12 steps). Add **`## 3. Preserve`** after Resolve: writes the source note, small-source-inline vs large-source-archive split (D1/D2), and a redaction gate SLOT emitted only when the tier's `gate:` is `public`/`shared` (D5), stating which destination assumption it was generated under. Add the orphaned-synthesis path to `## 2. Resolve` (D10). Add **`## 6. Synthesize`** as a conditional SLOT (like §11), proposing at 6 and writing during 8. State in Inspect that extraction reads raw, never the synthesis (D4). **Rewrite the `source-tiers` SLOT instruction** — it currently orders two tiers covering the same events as primary/secondary, which would generate the inversion into every new graph. |
  | `references/templates/knowledge-graph.md` | `## Types in This Graph` — synthesis from intermediate to sibling output (D3). |
  | `references/graph-conventions.md` | Same correction in "Entity and synthesis notes"; add that a synthesis note may be an adopted project artifact outside the graph root (D8). |
  | `references/deltas.md` | Six new entries at `version: 0.6.0` (below). |
  | `skills/graph-init/SKILL.md` | Block 2 q9b → sibling-output wording + "what produces the synthesis". Block 3 → new question on archive destination and whether it is public/shared. Step 4 → `archive:`/`gate:` sub-keys on a source tier, `produced-by:` on `types.synthesis`, synthesis `dir:` may sit outside the root. Step 5 → scaffold the raw archive dir; never create or overwrite an out-of-root synthesis dir. Step 6 → generation rules for the two new stages. **Sharpen the `Never` list** — per §5.4, "never invent `.commons.yml` keys beyond…" reads as constraining all keys; make it explicitly top-level. |
  | `README.md` | Synthesis row in the type table (line 78). |
  | `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` | `0.5.0` → `0.6.0` (minor — new capability, per `plugin-updates.md`). |
  | `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` | Same bump, matching. |
  | `scripts/scan-secrets.sh` | **New.** The deterministic floor (D6). Patterns: `-----BEGIN * PRIVATE KEY-----`, token prefixes (`sk-`, `sk-ant-`, `ghp_`, `gho_`, `github_pat_`, `xox[baprs]-`, `AKIA`), `KEY\|TOKEN\|SECRET\|PASSWORD\|CREDENTIAL` assignments with a non-placeholder value, `://user:pass@host`, `Authorization:` bearer values. Non-zero on any hit **and** non-zero on its own failure; prints each finding with file/line context so D7 can resolve them individually. |
  
  The six deltas, all anchored on headings verified to resolve exactly once in both the template
  and this project's generated skill (D11 — no delta renumbers a heading):
  
  | `id` | `file` | `anchor` |
  |---|---|---|
  | `preserve-stage` | process | `## 2. Resolve` |
  | `resolve-orphaned-synthesis` | process | `## 2. Resolve` |
  | `extract-from-raw` | process | `## 4. Inspect` |
  | `synthesis-in-plan` | process | `## 5. Propose the plan` |
  | `synthesis-write` | process | `## 6. Run to completion` |
  | `synthesis-is-sibling` | knowledge-graph | `## Types in This Graph` |
  
  Seven fields each. `extract-from-raw`'s `satisfied-test` written sharply per §5.5 — a section
  that merely *mentions* the synthesis must fail it. The log also records R3: after patching, an
  old graph and a fresh one are numbered differently, so future deltas targeting these stages
  anchor on the pre-existing heading until every live graph is patched.
  
  ## Phase 2 — BLOCKING: floor scan verification (§6 step 2, §7.1 first half)
  
  Runs the moment `scan-secrets.sh` exists. Nothing after this phase proceeds if it fails.
  Fixtures in `$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp`, never in the repo.
  
  - **Positive fixture** — a fake `sk-ant-` key, a fake private-key block, a `DATABASE_URL` with a
    password. **Pass:** exit non-zero **and** all three reported as *distinct* findings.
  - **Negative fixture** — `API_KEY=<your-key-here>` and prose mentioning `sk-`. **Pass:** exit
    zero.
  
  ## Phase 3 — This project's config (§6 step 3, §5.2)
  
  - `.commons.yml` — remove the `chronicle` source tier; add
    `types.synthesis: { name: chronicle, dir: docs/chronicle/, produced-by: meta-claude:session-chronicle }`;
    on the `session` tier replace `export-to:` with `archive: knowledge/sources/raw/` and
    `gate: public`. Edit surgically — hand-written comments survive byte-identical.
  - `.gitignore` — drop the `.claude/session-exports/` entry.
  - Create `knowledge/sources/raw/.gitkeep` (graph-init's own scaffold idiom for an empty type
    directory; nothing writes raw material into it this run).
  
  ## Phase 4 — Patch this project's generated skills (§6 step 4, §5.5)
  
  1. `/graph-patch`, pointed at the working-copy delta log (defect 2 above), per-delta approval,
     six deltas across `.claude/skills/process/SKILL.md` and `.claude/skills/knowledge-graph/SKILL.md`.

     patched skill contradicts itself.
  3. Also verify the `.claude/skills/knowledge-graph/SKILL.md` "Synthesis — none" line is now this
     graph's real synthesis tier (that is `synthesis-is-sibling`'s job; §5.2 states the outcome).
  
  ## Phase 5 — Graph notes (§6 step 5, §5.3)
  
  - Mark `knowledge/decisions/session transcripts are primary and the chronicle is the lossy fallback.md`
    superseded with a pointer at the top. **Content below unedited** (D12 + the global
    `keep-design-history` rule).
  - New: `knowledge/decisions/processing preserves the raw and synthesizes for a human, and extracts from the raw.md`
    — the four-stage model and why extraction reads raw.
  - `knowledge/maps/decisions.md` — alphabetical entry for the new decision with its one-clause
    "so what"; annotation on the superseded one.
  - `knowledge/sources/commons-scaffold session — …md` — add `raw: unavailable` with the reason
    (§6's closing note: the founding transcript is gone and the record should say so).
  
  ## Verification
  
  Run in this order; each gates what follows.
  
  1. **§7.1 first half** — Phase 2 above. Blocking.
  2. **§7.1 second half, criterion declared here, in advance.** After Phase 4, `chmod -x` the
     scan and run the patched project skill's preserve stage against a fixture input.
     **PASS iff both:** (a) the run reports the scan as unavailable and stops, naming it; and
     (b) `ls knowledge/sources/raw/` immediately afterward shows no file for that fixture. Any
     file written there, or a run that proceeds without naming the scan failure, is a **FAIL** —
     including a run that "notices" the problem only in retrospect. Then `chmod +x` and re-run to
     confirm the stage is not permanently broken.
  3. **§7.2 false-positive path** — copy the existing session export to
     `$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp`, run the gate over it, confirm hits are presented with surrounding
     context and are individually resolvable as redact / withhold / accept (D7).
  4. **§7.4 delta resolution** — confirm all six *pre-existing* deltas still resolve against the
     patched prose (D11's whole premise), and that each of the six new deltas' `satisfied-test`
     passes post-edit. `.commons.yml`'s `generated.process.applied` should end with twelve ids and
     `template-version: 0.6.0`.
  5. **JSON validity** — `plugin.json` and `marketplace.json` parse and carry matching `0.6.0`.
  
  ## Landing it
  
  Worktree off `main`, conventional commits, push, **draft** PR. Not docs-only, so this wants a
  PR rather than direct-to-main. Two repo rules are satisfied by construction and I'll say so in
  the PR body: every template edit carries its delta in the same commit
  (`.claude/rules/template-deltas.md`), and plugin + marketplace versions bump together
  (`.claude/rules/plugin-updates.md`).
  
  If any verification step fails, it goes in the PR body as a failure with its output — not as a
  softened caveat.

    entries = yaml.safe_load(block)

    ids = re.findall(r'^- id: (\S+)', block, re.M)

req = {"id","file","anchor","version","instruction","rationale","satisfied-test"}
print(f"{len(entries)} entries")

    extra = set(e) - req
    flag = "OK " if not missing and not extra else "BAD"
    print(f"  {flag} {e['id']:<28} file={e['file']:<15} v={e['version']} anchor={e['anchor']!r}"
          + (f" MISSING={missing}" if missing else "") + (f" EXTRA={extra}" if extra else ""))

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/derek-personal/Developer/claude-code-plugins/.claude/worktrees/knowledge-commons-0.6.0-pipeline
git commit -q -F - <<'MSG'
feat(knowledge-commons): add the preserve and synthesize stages to the pipeline

The generated /process pipeline was modeled on a four-stage flow — take in,
preserve, synthesize, extract from the raw — and two of those stages were never
in the template. Nothing wrote a source note (one step searched for one, another
stamped one); nothing produced a synthesis, though the role was specified into
the conventions, the knowledge-graph template, the interview, and the README.

The absence is not theoretical. With no preserve stage, preservation degraded to
a pointer into a gitignored directory, and this graph's founding source note
already points at a transcript that no longer exists.

Because the pipeline could not produce a synthesis, graphs put the synthesis on
the input side as a second source tier, and grew a primary/fallback ordering and
a double-counting guard to contain it. The corrected model makes synthesis and
extraction siblings of the raw material, so none of that machinery is needed.

- process template: new Preserve (3) and Synthesize (6) stages; extraction reads
  the raw material and never the synthesis; orphaned-synthesis path for raw that
  is verifiably gone; the source-tiers SLOT no longer instructs the generator to
  rank two tiers covering the same events, which would have generated the
  inversion into every new graph
- redaction gate on the preserve stage when the archive destination is public or
  shared: scripts/scan-secrets.sh as a deterministic floor that fails closed on
  its own failure as well as on a hit, plus a subagent read-through; per-hit
  redact / withhold / accept; withhold when no human can resolve them
- graph-conventions + knowledge-graph template + README: synthesis corrected from
  extraction intermediate to sibling output, and may be an adopted project
  artifact outside the graph root
- graph-init: archive/gate questions, produced-by on the synthesis tier, scaffolds
  the archive dir, never touches an out-of-root synthesis dir, and its "never
  invent keys" line now says top-level, which is what it always meant
- six delta entries so the corrections reach already-generated graphs, all
  anchored on pre-0.6.0 headings so none of the six existing anchors break

The floor's planted-secret test ships as test-scan-secrets.sh rather than being
performed once: its first run reported three findings and silently missed a
planted private key, because a pattern beginning with "-" was parsed as grep
options, grep exited 2, and stderr was discarded — a miss shaped exactly like a
clean pass.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

p = "knowledge/changelog.md"
s = open(p).read()
entry = """## 2026-07

### 2026-07-28 — pipeline correction: preserve and synthesize (knowledge-commons 0.6.0)

Not a `/process` run. A structural correction to the pipeline itself, implemented from
`docs/specs/knowledge-commons-process-pipeline.md`, plus the graph notes recording it.

**Changed**
- Decisions (1 new, 1 superseded): `processing preserves the raw and synthesizes for a human, and
  extracts from the raw` records the four-stage model. `session transcripts are primary and the
  chronicle is the lossy fallback` is marked superseded with a pointer; its content is unedited.
- Source (1): the founding source note gains `raw: unavailable` — its transcript is gone, verified
  absent rather than assumed, and the record should say so.
- `maps/decisions.md`: entry for the new decision, supersession marker on the old one.
- `.commons.yml`: the chronicle moves from `sources:` to `types.synthesis` (adopted at
  `docs/chronicle/`, outside the graph root); the session tier gains a committed
  `archive: knowledge/sources/raw/` and `gate: public`.
- Both generated skills patched through six template deltas.

**Decided**
- The chronicle is this pipeline's output, not a second input tier. Everything the old arrangement
  needed — a primary/fallback ordering, an overlapping-tier guard, a transcript-survival check — was
  machinery containing that one inversion, and all of it is gone.
- Raw material is archived in-repo and committed. The gitignored-scratch predecessor is what let the
  founding transcript vanish with nothing announcing the loss.

**Learned**
- The redaction floor's first run reported three findings and silently missed a planted private key:
  a pattern starting with `-` was eaten as grep options, grep exited 2, and stderr was discarded, so

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/derek-personal/Developer/claude-code-plugins/.claude/worktrees/knowledge-commons-0.6.0-pipeline
gh pr create --draft --title "feat(knowledge-commons): add the preserve and synthesize stages (0.6.0)" --body "$(cat <<'BODY'
Implements `docs/specs/knowledge-commons-process-pipeline.md` (v1.2), §6 steps 1–5 plus §7.1, §7.2, §7.4.

The `/process` pipeline was modeled on a four-stage flow — take in, preserve, synthesize, extract from the raw — and two of those stages were never in the template. Nothing wrote a source note (one step searched for one, another stamped one). Nothing produced a synthesis, though the role was specified into the conventions, the knowledge-graph template, the interview, and the README.

The absence is not theoretical: with no preserve stage, preservation degraded to a pointer into a gitignored directory, and this graph's founding source note already points at a transcript that no longer exists.

Because the pipeline could not *produce* a synthesis, this graph put its synthesis on the **input** side — the chronicle registered as a second source tier — and grew a primary/fallback ordering, an overlapping-tiers guard, and a transcript-survival check to contain it. All of that is gone; none of it is needed once the synthesis sits on the output side.

## Scope

**Out:** §6 step 6 / §7.3, the end-to-end `/process` run on the authoring session. Derek ran that separately. §5.3's `observations/` row goes with it, since the spec says those are written by the run rather than hand-authored.

## Verification

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| §7.1 planted secret | **PASS** — exit 1, all three planted secrets as three distinct findings |
| §7.1 negative fixture | **PASS** — exit 0 on `API_KEY=<your-key-here>` and prose mentioning `sk-` |
| §7.1 broken scan (criterion declared in advance) | **PASS** — floor exited 126, stage stopped, `knowledge/sources/raw/` still holds only `.gitkeep` |
| §7.2 false-positive path | **PASS** — 3 hits on a real 100 KB export, each with file:line and context, each individually resolvable |
| §7.4 pre-existing anchors | **PASS** — all 6 still resolve exactly once in the patched prose |
| §7.4 new deltas | **PASS** — all 6 `satisfied-test`s verified post-edit; 12/12 stamped |
| JSON validity + version parity | **PASS** — plugin and marketplace both 0.6.0 |

**The planted-secret test earned its keep.** Its first run reported three findings and silently missed the planted private key: a pattern beginning with `-` was parsed as grep options, grep exited 2, and `2>/dev/null` discarded the error — a miss shaped exactly like a clean pass. An exit-code-only assertion stayed green through all of it. The scan now fails closed when it cannot run, and the test ships as `test-scan-secrets.sh` rather than having been performed once. Re-running the suite against the reintroduced bug turns it red.

**The false-positive path produced a real result.** All three hits on the session export were prose describing that same test fixture. Blanket-withholding would have discarded a 100 KB transcript over three lines of documentation — which is the D7 argument, demonstrated rather than asserted.

## Repo rules

- `.claude/rules/template-deltas.md` — every template edit carries its delta in the same commit. Six new entries, all anchored on headings that already exist in generated prose, so none of the six pre-existing anchors break (D11). The log records the resulting numbering divergence (R3).
- `.claude/rules/plugin-updates.md` — plugin and marketplace bumped together, `0.5.0` → `0.6.0`. Minor, not patch: users gain preservation, synthesis, and the redaction gate.

## Defects found in the spec

1. **Five vs six deltas.** §5.5 enumerates six and explains the v1.1 split of `synthesis-stage`; §5.1, §5.2, §6 step 4 and §7.4 still say "five". Went with six.
2. **§5.4's numbering table drops the Sinks stage.** It lists 12 sections and omits `Sinks`, which exists in the template between Run-to-completion and Continue-and-collect. Nothing in the spec asks to remove it and N1 protects the working steps, so it was kept — the template is 13 sections, not 12. Deleting a working stage the spec never mentions would have silently dropped task routing from every newly generated graph.
3. **`/graph-patch` cannot see new deltas.** It resolves `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` to the installed cache (0.5.0), which has six ids where the working copy has twelve. Reading it would have reported "nothing pending" — a confirmed clean bill of health, the exact failure `step10-pending-patches` warns about. Patched against the working-copy log instead; the global cache was left alone, since three other projects install from it.
4. **§5.2 names one unanchored hand edit; there were three.** The step-3 overlapping-tiers guard (called out), plus the step-1 chronicle queue and the skill's own frontmatter description, which ranked the two tiers. The delta's `satisfied-test` cannot pass while those stand, so all three landed in that delta's commit.
5. **§7.2 says `.claude/session-exports/` is empty.** It holds a 99 KB export of the authoring session.
6. **Not fixed, flagged:** `docs/specs/knowledge-commons.md` §5's `.commons.yml` sketch still comments synthesis as "intermediate for rich bounded sources" — the framing D3 corrects. §5.1 doesn't list the parent spec.

## Two deviations worth your eye

- **Per-delta approval was deferred to this PR.** graph-patch requires `y`/`edit`/`skip` per delta and forbids batch approval. In a background run that stalls indefinitely, so each delta is its own commit — the review surface the gate exists to buy, moved to where you can actually use it.
- **One coherence fix is its own commit** (`118415e`), separate from the deltas on purpose. graph-patch's step 6 reports coherence concerns rather than fixing them. This one was not just stale naming: "Extraction Workflow: chronicle" told a writing session the canonical `source:` was the entry's repo-relative path, which is the wrong ledger key and would mint a duplicate source note. Standing on its own for review rather than folded into an approved delta.

## 3. Findings

### The scanner — direct answer to your question

**Yes, I fed it credential shapes it claims to catch. It exits 0 with a real secret present, by two independent routes.**

---

## C1 — CONFIRMED. Quoting a credential defeats the assignment scan entirely
`plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh:58` (PLACEHOLDER), `:154` (the test)

`PLACEHOLDER` includes `"` and `'` in its leading character class, and `:154` tests the extracted value against `^$PLACEHOLDER`. Any value beginning with a quote is classified as a placeholder and dropped.

Executed:
```
DB_PASSWORD=Tr0ub4dor3notaplaceholder       → exit 1  (finding)
DB_PASSWORD="Tr0ub4dor&3-notaplaceholder"   → exit 0  CLEAN
DB_PASSWORD='Tr0ub4dor3notaplaceholder'     → exit 0  CLEAN
export API_KEY="sk-live-9f8a7b6c5d4e3f2a1b0c"        → exit 0  CLEAN
SECRET_TOKEN='[REDACTED-FIXTURE-GITHUB]'  → exit 0  CLEAN
```

Adding quotes turns a hit into a clean pass. Scope: this kills exactly the class the assignment pattern exists for — credentials with no recognized prefix. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-…"` still trips, but only because the `sk-ant-` pattern fires independently. Every quoted `.env`, YAML, shell-export or JS-const secret without a known prefix passes clean.

The comment at `:55-57` describes the intent — bracketed values, `$VAR`, `{{var}}`, dummy words. `"` and `'` are not that, and their inclusion inverts the gate for the most common way people write secrets.

## C2 — CONFIRMED. The fail-closed guard on scanner failure cannot fire
`scan-secrets.sh:92-100` (guard at `:96-98`), called from `:127` and `:144`

`grep_lines` is invoked as `out="$(grep_lines …)"`. `die` runs `exit 2` **inside the command substitution's subshell**. The main shell continues; the assignment's status is discarded; `out` is empty; `[ -z "$out" ] && continue` reads it as no match.

Structural proof:
```
$ bash repro.sh
DIE: simulated grep failure
STILL RUNNING. out='[]'
repro rc=0
```

Against the real script, with a malformed ERE planted the way a maintainer adding a pattern would, on a file containing only a secret that pattern would have caught:
```
scan-secrets: scanning 1 file(s)
grep: braces not balanced
scan-secrets: grep failed (exit 2) while scanning o.txt for: sk_live_[A-Za-z0-9(]{20,

scan-secrets: clean — no credential shapes found.
RC=0
```

**A pattern that failed to run yields exit 0 "clean."** This is the exact failure the comment at `:88-91` and `test-scan-secrets.sh:12-17` say this code was written to fix. `set -uo pipefail` at `:22` doesn't help — there is no `set -e`, and even with it a command substitution's status isn't checked here. The header contract at `:14-20` ("`2` the scan could not run… A scan that cannot run is not a pass") holds only for the preflight at `:35-46`, which is in the main shell. Every failure discovered *during* scanning degrades to clean-or-partial.

## C3 — CONFIRMED. The test suite does not go red on a real regression
`plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/test-scan-secrets.sh:73-76`

I deleted the entire credential-assignment scan — one of the five shapes the gate advertises, and the only one with placeholder logic — and reran:
```
ok — reports: private key block
ok — reports: Anthropic API key
ok — reports: connection string carrying a password
ok — exactly 3 distinct findings, no duplicates
ok — negative fixture exits zero
ok — no arguments exits 2 / missing file exits 2 / directory exits 2
all cases passed.
```
All nine assertions green. The positive fixture's three plants are each caught by a *prefix* pattern, so `count -eq 3` is satisfied without the assignment path existing. No case covers a credential lacking a known prefix, a quoted value, or a scanner-internal failure — which is why C2 survived. The suite proves three regexes exist; it does not test the gate.

  local name="$1" mutate="$2"

  cp "$S" "$d/scan-secrets.sh"; cp "$TS" "$d/test-scan-secrets.sh"

  printf "  %-34s suite rc=%s  %s\n" "$name" "$rc" "$(printf '%s' "$out" | grep -c 'FAIL') failing case(s)"
}

# M6: the divergence note documented only the forward direction.
p = "plugins/knowledge-commons/references/deltas.md"
s = open(p).read()
old = """**Future deltas targeting the preserve or synthesize stages must anchor on the pre-existing heading,
not on the new stage's own heading** — `## 2. Resolve` rather than `## 3. Preserve` — until this log
records that every live graph has been patched through 0.6.0. A delta anchored on `## 3. Preserve`
resolves in a freshly generated graph and silently fails to resolve in every patched one, which is the
failure this log's skip-loudly rule exists to make visible rather than to rely on."""
new = """**Future deltas targeting the preserve or synthesize stages must anchor on the pre-existing heading,
not on the new stage's own heading** — `## 2. Resolve` rather than `## 3. Preserve` — until this log
records that every live graph has been patched through 0.6.0. A delta anchored on `## 3. Preserve`
resolves in a freshly generated graph and silently fails to resolve in every patched one, which is the
failure this log's skip-loudly rule exists to make visible rather than to rely on.

**The divergence runs in both directions, and the reverse arm is the easier one to forget.** Nine of the
twelve anchors above — every pre-0.6.0 `process` entry, plus the three new ones anchored on
`## 4. Inspect`, `## 5. Propose the plan` and `## 6. Run to completion` — name headings the 0.6.0
template no longer has, because inserting two stages renumbered everything after `## 2. Resolve`. They
still resolve in every *already-generated* graph, which is the population they exist to patch, so this
is not a defect in them.

What it means in practice: **`applied:` is what protects a freshly generated graph, not anchor
resolution.** `graph-init` stamps every current delta id as applied at generation time precisely because
the prose is rendered from current templates, so those deltas are never selected for it. Two paths can
still reach an anchor that will not resolve — `/graph-patch`'s 0.1.8 bootstrap, and a retry after a
delta failed and stayed unstamped. Both skip loudly and neither misapplies, but a reader seeing
`anchor not found` on a 0.6.0-generated graph should recognize it as this, not as a corrupted skill."""
assert s.count(old) == 1
s = s.replace(old, new)
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("M8 disambiguated; M6 reverse direction documented")
PY)

Implements `docs/specs/knowledge-commons-process-pipeline.md` (v1.2), §6 steps 1–5 plus §7.1, §7.2, §7.4.

The `/process` pipeline was modeled on a four-stage flow — take in, preserve, synthesize, extract from the raw — and two of those stages were never in the template. Nothing wrote a source note (one step searched for one, another stamped one). Nothing produced a synthesis, though the role was specified into the conventions, the knowledge-graph template, the interview, and the README.

The absence is not theoretical: with no preserve stage, preservation degraded to a pointer into a gitignored directory, and this graph's founding source note already points at a transcript that no longer exists.

Because the pipeline could not *produce* a synthesis, this graph put its synthesis on the **input** side — the chronicle registered as a second source tier — and grew a primary/fallback ordering, an overlapping-tiers guard, and a transcript-survival check to contain it. All of that is gone; none of it is needed once the synthesis sits on the output side.

**Out of scope:** §6 step 6 / §7.3, the end-to-end `/process` run on the authoring session. Derek ran that separately. §5.3's `observations/` row goes with it, since the spec says those are written by the run rather than hand-authored. **The four-stage pipeline has therefore not been executed end to end in this branch** — worth knowing when reading the verification table below.

## Correction: an earlier version of this PR overclaimed

The first version of this description said the redaction floor "fails closed on its own failure." **That was wrong**, and an independent review that executed the scanner rather than reading it found three defects (`32291ae` fixes all three):

1. **Quoting a credential made it invisible.** `PLACEHOLDER` had `"` and `'` in its leading character class, so the value extracted from `KEY="realsecret"` began with a quote, matched the placeholder test, and was dropped. Every quoted `.env`, YAML, JSON or shell secret without a recognized prefix passed clean.
2. **A pattern that failed to compile produced "clean" and exit 0** with the secret still in the file. The failure handler ran inside a command substitution, so its `exit 2` killed only the subshell and the caller read the empty result as "no match". My §7.1 break-it test used `chmod -x`, which fails *before* the script runs — so it exercised the one failure class that did work, and I generalized from it.
3. **The suite stayed green through both**, and through deleting the entire credential-assignment scan. All three planted secrets were also caught by prefix patterns, so "exactly 3 findings" held with that path gone. It proved three regexes existed; it did not test the gate.

Also found and fixed: JSON-shaped credentials were invisible *while JSONL is the input format*; lowercase `password:` and current `sk-proj-` keys were missed; a placeholder bearer token was a false positive; the export was written into the committed archive and scanned afterwards, so "withhold" meant deleting a file rather than never writing one.

## Repo rules

- `.claude/rules/template-deltas.md` — six new delta entries, all anchored on headings that already exist in generated prose, so none of the six pre-existing anchors break (D11).
- `.claude/rules/plugin-updates.md` — plugin and marketplace bumped together, `0.5.0` → `0.6.0`, minor.

They resolve in every already-generated graph, which is the population deltas exist to patch, and `graph-init` stamps all current ids on a fresh graph so they are never selected there. The delta log now documents both directions of this divergence; the spec's §5.5 claim that every anchor resolves "in both the template and this project's generated skill" is false for 3 of the 6 new entries.

## Defects found in the spec

1. **Five vs six deltas.** §5.5 enumerates six and explains the v1.1 split; §5.1, §5.2, §6 step 4 and §7.4 still say "five". Went with six.
2. **§5.4's numbering table drops the Sinks stage.** It lists 12 sections and omits `Sinks`, which exists between Run-to-completion and Continue-and-collect. Nothing asks to remove it and N1 protects the working steps, so it was kept — the template is 13 sections. Following the table literally would have silently dropped task routing from every newly generated graph.
3. **`/graph-patch` cannot see new deltas.** It resolves `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` to the installed 0.5.0 cache, which has six ids where the working copy has twelve. Reading it would have reported "nothing pending" — the exact failure `step10-pending-patches` warns about. Patched against the working-copy log; the global cache was left alone, since three other projects install from it.
4. **§5.2 names one unanchored hand edit; there were three.** The step-3 guard (called out), plus the step-1 chronicle queue and the frontmatter description, which ranked the two tiers.
5. **§7.2 says `.claude/session-exports/` is empty.** It holds a 99 KB export of the authoring session.
6. **Not fixed, flagged:** `docs/specs/knowledge-commons.md` §5's `.commons.yml` sketch still calls synthesis an "intermediate for rich bounded sources" — the framing D3 corrects. §5.1 doesn't list the parent spec.

## Two deviations worth your eye

- **Per-delta approval was deferred to this PR.** graph-patch requires `y`/`edit`/`skip` per delta and forbids batch approval. In a background run that stalls indefinitely, so each delta is its own commit — the review surface the gate exists to buy, moved to where you can use it.
- **Two commits are deliberately outside the delta set.** `118415e` retargets "Extraction Workflow: chronicle", which told a writing session the canonical `source:` was a repo-relative path — the wrong ledger key, which would mint duplicate source notes. `32291ae` is the scanner fix round. graph-patch reports coherence concerns rather than folding them into approved deltas, so both stand alone.

## Accepted, not fixed

- `MONKEY=bananas12` is a finding (`KEY` matches inside `MONKEY`). A false positive is the safe direction for this gate and is cheap to resolve per-hit.
- `PASSWORD=$ecretPassw0rd` reads clean — a leading `$` is treated as a template reference, which is the right default far more often than not.

# Independent pass over `32291ae`

**Verdict: the fix is sound.** All three original defects are genuinely closed — I re-ran every input that previously exited 0 and every one now exits 1. The C2 fix is structurally right, not just empirically: `scan_file` is invoked from the main shell at `:228`, the greps are inlined at `:162/164/181/203` with `rc=$?` read in the caller's shell, so `die` at `:167/183/205` actually terminates the process. ---

## N1 — CONFIRMED. A `,` `;` `}` or space in the first 8 characters of a value hides it
`scan-secrets.sh:98` (`[^[:space:],;}]+` truncates the match), `:195` (`[ "${#value}" -lt 8 ]` then drops it)

```
PASSWORD="abcdefgh,ijklmnop"              → exit 1   (delimiter at char 9)
PASSWORD="abcdefg,hijklmnop"              → exit 0   (delimiter at char 8)
PASSWORD=Tr0ub4d,or3xyz                   → exit 0
{"db_password":"a;bcdefghijklmnop"}       → exit 0
PASSPHRASE="correct horse battery staple" → exit 0
```

The regex truncates the value at the first delimiter, then the 8-char floor discards the stub. The passphrase case is the one I'd weight: `PASSPHRASE` is in the keyword list at `:98`, and a passphrase contains spaces by definition — the keyword is advertising a shape the value regex cannot carry.

Worth stating the trade-off honestly rather than as a bug report: **the 8-char floor is also what keeps the false-positive rate down** (below). Loosening one tightens the other. Splitting them — e.g. measure length before truncation, or allow a quoted value to span delimiters up to the closing quote — is the shape that gets both, but it's a real design call, not a typo.

## N2 — CONFIRMED. Same-line dedup drops a second distinct secret, and can mislabel the one it keeps
`scan-secrets.sh:120` — `report()` now calls `already_reported` unconditionally

Previously only the generic assignment path deferred; the old comment said "Two *specific* shapes on one line still earn two findings; only the generic pattern defers." That distinction is gone.

```
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer ghp_AAAA…" -d "slack=[REDACTED-FIXTURE-SLACK]"
→ 1 finding (GitHub token). The Slack token is not reported.
```

Worse on a long line, which is what a rendered transcript is made of. A 365-char line with `AKIA…` at the start and `xoxb-…` at the end:

```
[1] Slack token (xox*)
    long.txt:1
    > AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE the assistant then explained at length what had happened during the run, …
```

The label names the Slack token; the 200-char context shows only the AWS key. **The reported secret is invisible and the visible secret is unlabeled.** Under D7 ("each hit surfaced with surrounding context and resolved individually") that line cannot be correctly resolved from the report — redacting what you can see leaves the reported one in place.

**Tempering this honestly:** on a real 4.7 MB / 2,098-line session transcript, the dedup suppressed **zero** findings — 11 with it, 11 without. Confirmed defect, no observed occurrence in the one real sample I had.

## Pre-existing, still live
`scan-secrets.sh:62` — `$` is a leading placeholder marker, so `PASSWORD=$uperSecretValue123` → exit 0. I filed this as L5 last time; unchanged, still Low.

---

## False-positive floodgate: nothing in this class

You were right to ask, and the answer is no. Measured three ways:

| corpus | findings |
|---|---|
| 127 `.md` files across `docs/`, `knowledge/`, `plugins/`, `.claude/skills/` | **1** |
| a real 4.7 MB session transcript (`.jsonl`) | **11**, all assignment-shaped |
| a deliberately adversarial 11-line prose file | **2** |

The two adversarial hits were `The key: understanding that a synthesis is a sibling output.` and `Set MONKEY_BARS = playground equipment`. The reason it isn't worse is the 8-char floor: `The secret: nobody…`, `- Token: the smallest…`, `monkey: bananas…`, `The password: hunter2…` all produce a value under 8 chars and are dropped. 11 hits per transcript is a workable per-run review load under D7. `-i` did not open a floodgate.

(I ran the transcript scan read-only and printed labels and counts only, never matched context.)

## Test suite

28 cases, green as shipped. I mutated the scanner 15 ways, including several the file's own rationale doesn't anticipate.

**RED — caught:** delete the assignment block (7 cases), delete the Authorization block (2), drop `-i` from the assignment grep (2), drop the quote-tolerance `["']?` from `ASSIGN_RE` (1), value floor 8→40 (7), remove the JWT pattern (1), weaken `-gt 1` to `-gt 2` (1), make `die` exit 0 (3), full C1 revert — both halves (5).

**GREEN — blind spots:**
- `report-drop-sameline-dedup` — dedup is unasserted. Moot given N2.
- `drop-readable-preflight` — benign: grep then fails and the inlined rc check catches it at `:167`.
- `extract-drop-quote-strip` alone, and `placeholder-readd-quotes` alone — **benign**. Either half alone prevents C1, so a single-half mutation leaves the gate correct, and reverting both goes red with 5 failures. That's defense-in-depth working, not a gap. Worth one comment line so a future maintainer doesn't remove the "redundant" one.
- Multi-file invocation is untested. I verified it works (clean file + dirty file → exit 1; a missing second argument fails closed at exit 2 before scanning the first).

**Fixture splitting — confirmed safe.** `"xox""b-…"` is adjacent-string concatenation joined at parse time, then written via `printf '%s\n' "$content"`. I dumped what the scanner actually receives and fed each split fixture in standalone:

```
[REDACTED-FIXTURE-SLACK]              → [1] Slack token (xox*)
pasted [REDACTED-FIXTURE-STRIPE]…     → [1] Stripe key (sk_live/rk_live)
pasted sk-proj-abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz…     → [1] OpenAI-style API key (sk-)
pasted [REDACTED-FIXTURE-ANTHROPIC]…   → [1] Anthropic API key (sk-ant-)
token [REDACTED-FIXTURE-GITHUB] → [1] GitHub token
```

Contiguous, and each trips its own labeled pattern. The splitting weakened nothing.

The malformed-pattern injection at `:141` also works, and works for the right reason: the broken pattern lands at index 6, ahead of the real Stripe pattern at `:85`, so `die` fires first. If C2 regressed, the scan would continue, reach Stripe, report, and exit 1 — the test wants 2, so it goes red. Correctly wired.
*Nit:* the comment at `:138-139` says "the only secret in the file is one that the broken pattern was the one to catch." Not true — the real Stripe pattern catches it too. The assertion is right; the rationale overstates.

**Edge sweep, all correct:** secret on the last line with no trailing newline → 1; CRLF → 1; leading-dash filename → 1; binary/NUL/invalid-UTF-8 content → 1; directory, missing file, no args → 2.

**Paths where a scan that did not fully run reports clean: nothing in this class.** I probed malformed regex, unreadable file, missing file, directory, no-args, multi-file with one missing, leading-dash filename, and binary content. Every one exits 1 or 2. C2 is closed.

---

## The two prose fixes

**M4 — closes the hole, one residual.** Template `:161-166` and generated `:161-166` both require scratch → scan → move, and generated `:85` pre-empts it at the resolve step so a top-down reader hits the constraint before the archive path. Residual: generated `.claude/skills/process/SKILL.md:126` still reads "The rendered export **is written to** `knowledge/sources/raw/`, committed with the repo" — 28 lines before the gate, 35 before the scratch instruction, unqualified. It's a form description and `:85` covers it, but it's the one sentence that still says the opposite. Same at `templates/process.md:119-120`, where the `preserve-form` SLOT says "the rendered form is what gets archived and scanned" without the ordering. Both Low.

**M7 — lands in the template, but `graph-init` now contradicts it. This is the one live regression path.**

`templates/process.md:302-306` now carries "**The plan names the synthesis work too.**" with both branches and the approval framing — satisfies the delta's satisfied-test as written. ✓

But `skills/graph-init/SKILL.md:403-407` was not updated and still reads:

> **The synthesize section and its write paragraph are conditional on `types.synthesis`,** and they are conditional *together*. Generate **both or neither**…

Two pieces, when there are now three. The new SLOT comment at `templates/process.md:301-303` says "all three or none" — but graph-init step 6 is where a generator looks for generation rules, and it enumerates only the section and the write paragraph. **A generator following graph-init literally omits the plan paragraph for a synthesis-declaring graph, reproducing M7 in every freshly generated skill.** Medium.

---

## M8 — I withdraw it. Your reasoning is right and mine was wrong.

Two separate reasons, and the second is the interesting one.

First, mechanically: the SLOT prohibition is conditioned on *two tiers covering the same underlying events*. Orchard has one. The example never triggers the rule I said it contradicted. I was matching on the word "chronicle" rather than reading the condition.

Second, substantively: your test — a document is a synthesis when some other artifact recorded the same event first — is the correct operational form of D3. A synthesis is a sibling *of raw material*; where there is no raw material there is nothing for it to be a sibling of, so the logbook is simply the record. Adding the disambiguating note rather than rewriting a valid example was the better call, and the note is more useful than the example change would have been because it gives the generator a test instead of a taboo.

One residual, explicitly non-blocking: the test asks what *exists*, and the interview only sees what's *declared*. A project that keeps a chronicle whose sessions do have transcripts, but whose interview never surfaced them, satisfies "nothing underneath it" in the config while it's false in the world — and regenerates the inversion. Your added "Ask which one the material is before placing the tier" mostly covers this; widening it to "ask whether anything else recorded the same event, whether or not it was declared as a tier" would close it.

---

N1 and N2 are worth fixing but neither is the shape of the original three — they are narrow misses, not a gate reporting clean while broken. **M7's graph-init gap is the one I'd fix before merge**, because it silently undoes a fix you already made, in generated output nobody will re-review.

python3 - <<'PY'
p = "plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh"
s = open(p).read()

# --- N1: let a quoted value span delimiters, up to its closing quote --------------
old = """readonly ASSIGN_RE='[A-Za-z0-9_.-]*(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|PASSWD|PASSPHRASE|CREDENTIAL)[A-Za-z0-9_.-]*["'"'"']?[[:space:]]*[:=][[:space:]]*["'"'"']?[^[:space:],;}]+'"""
new = """readonly ASSIGN_RE='[A-Za-z0-9_.-]*(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|PASSWD|PASSPHRASE|CREDENTIAL)[A-Za-z0-9_.-]*["'"'"']?[[:space:]]*[:=][[:space:]]*("[^"]*"|'"'"'[^'"'"']*'"'"'|[^[:space:],;}]+)'"""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "ASSIGN_RE"
s = s.replace(old, new)

old = """extract_value() {
  printf '%s' "$1" | sed -E 's/^[^:=]*[:=][[:space:]]*//; s/^["'"'"']//'
}"""
new = """# Strip the assignment's name and separator, then the surrounding quotes, leaving the
# value. A QUOTED value is captured whole by ASSIGN_RE, delimiters and spaces included:
# truncating at the first comma or space and then applying the 8-character floor below
# discarded real credentials — PASSPHRASE="correct horse battery staple" measured as
# "correct" and was dropped, while the keyword list advertises a shape that by
# definition contains spaces.
extract_value() {
  printf '%s' "$1" | sed -E 's/^[^:=]*[:=][[:space:]]*//; s/^["'"'"']//; s/["'"'"']$//'
}"""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "extract_value"
s = s.replace(old, new)

# --- N2: accumulate every shape found on a line, instead of keeping only the first --
old = """findings=0
# Lines already reported, as "|<file>:<lineno>|" segments. Bash 3.2 (the macOS system
# shell) has no associative arrays, so membership is a substring test on one string.
reported_lines="|"

already_reported() {
  case "$reported_lines" in
    *"|$1:$2|"*) return 0 ;;
    *) return 1 ;;
  esac
}

# Print one finding. Truncates the context line so a long transcript line does not
# flood the report; the file:line reference is what a reader follows to see the rest.
report() {
  local label="$1" file="$2" lineno="$3" text="$4"
  already_reported "$file" "$lineno" && return 0
  findings=$((findings + 1))
  reported_lines="${reported_lines}${file}:${lineno}|"
  printf '\\n[%d] %s\\n' "$findings" "$label"
  printf '    %s:%s\\n' "$file" "$lineno"
  printf '    > %.200s\\n' "$text"
}"""
new = """# Findings accumulate as three parallel arrays keyed on "<file>:<lineno>" — bash 3.2
# (the macOS system shell) has no associative arrays.
#
# One finding per LINE, carrying EVERY shape found on it. Reporting one line once keeps
# the report resolvable under D7, since a reader redacts or withholds a line as a unit.
# Carrying every label is the other half, and it is not cosmetic: a line holding a
# GitHub token and a Slack token reported under one label, with a context window that
# may not even show the labelled one, cannot be resolved correctly from the report —
# you redact what you can see and leave the reported secret in place.
F_KEYS=()
F_LABELS=()
F_TEXTS=()

# Index of an already-recorded line, printed on stdout; non-zero if absent.
find_finding() {
  local key="$1" i=0
  while [ "$i" -lt "${#F_KEYS[@]}" ]; do
    if [ "${F_KEYS[$i]}" = "$key" ]; then printf '%s' "$i"; return 0; fi
    i=$((i + 1))
  done
  return 1
}

report() {
  local label="$1" file="$2" lineno="$3" text="$4" key idx
  key="${file}:${lineno}"
  if idx="$(find_finding "$key")"; then
    case "; ${F_LABELS[$idx]}; " in
      *"; $label; "*) ;;                                    # already noted on this line
      *) F_LABELS[$idx]="${F_LABELS[$idx]}; $label" ;;
    esac
  else
    F_KEYS+=("$key")
    F_LABELS+=("$label")
    F_TEXTS+=("$text")
  fi
}"""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "report block"
s = s.replace(old, new)

# The two value-checked paths no longer short-circuit on an already-reported line —
# their label is real information about that line even when another pattern hit it.
s = s.replace("""      text="${line#*:}"
      already_reported "$file" "$lineno" && continue
      value="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$ASSIGN_RE" | head -1)\"""",
              """      text="${line#*:}"
      value="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$ASSIGN_RE" | head -1)\"""")
s = s.replace("""      text="${line#*:}"
      already_reported "$file" "$lineno" && continue
      value="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$AUTH_RE" | head -1 \\""",
              """      text="${line#*:}"
      value="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$AUTH_RE" | head -1 \\""")

# --- emit ------------------------------------------------------------------------
old = """if [ "$findings" -eq 0 ]; then
  printf '\\nscan-secrets: clean — no credential shapes found.\\n'
  exit "$EXIT_CLEAN"
fi

printf '\\nscan-secrets: %d finding(s). Resolve each one individually — redact, withhold, or accept as a false positive — before anything is archived.\\n' "$findings"
exit "$EXIT_HITS\""""
new = """if [ "${#F_KEYS[@]}" -eq 0 ]; then
  printf '\\nscan-secrets: clean — no credential shapes found.\\n'
  exit "$EXIT_CLEAN"
fi

i=0
while [ "$i" -lt "${#F_KEYS[@]}" ]; do
  printf '\\n[%d] %s\\n' "$((i + 1))" "${F_LABELS[$i]}"
  printf '    %s\\n' "${F_KEYS[$i]}"
  # Truncated so one long transcript line does not flood the report; the file:line
  # reference is what a reader follows to see the rest.
  printf '    > %.200s\\n' "${F_TEXTS[$i]}"
  i=$((i + 1))
done

printf '\\nscan-secrets: %d finding(s). Resolve each one individually — redact, withhold, or accept as a false positive — before anything is archived.\\n' "${#F_KEYS[@]}"
exit "$EXIT_HITS\""""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "emit block"
s = s.replace(old, new)
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("N1 + N2 applied")

p = "plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/test-scan-secrets.sh"

# The overstated rationale the reviewer caught: the real Stripe pattern catches it too.
old = """# The regression test for defect (2) above. A maintainer adding a pattern typos the
# regex; grep exits 2 on it. The scan must stop, NOT report clean — even though the
# only secret in the file is one that the broken pattern was the one to catch."""
new = """# The regression test for defect (2) above. A maintainer adding a pattern typos the
# regex; grep exits 2 on it. The scan must stop rather than continue.
#
# The broken pattern is injected AHEAD of the real Stripe pattern, which is what makes
# this assertion sharp: if the fail-closed check regressed, the scan would carry on,
# reach the real pattern, report the secret and exit 1 — so the test wants 2, and 1 is
# a failure rather than an acceptable near-miss. (The file's secret is caught by the
# real pattern too; the ordering, not the secret's uniqueness, is what this pins.)"""
assert s.count(old) == 1
s = s.replace(old, new)

# New cases for the two holes the second review found, plus the untested multi-file path.
old = """# --- 2. Things that must NOT trip it ---------------------------------------------"""
new = """# A quoted value is captured whole, delimiters and spaces included. Truncating it at the
# first comma or space and then applying the 8-character floor discarded real credentials.
printf '\\nquoted values spanning delimiters\\n'

expect_exit "passphrase with spaces"            1 'PASSPHRASE="correct horse battery staple"'
expect_exit "comma inside the first 8 chars"    1 'PASSWORD="abcdefg,hijklmnop"'
expect_exit "semicolon inside a JSON value"     1 '{"db_password":"a;bcdefghijklmnop"}'

# One line, several shapes: one finding carrying every label. Reporting only the first
# leaves a reader redacting what the context window happens to show while the labelled
# secret stays in the file.
printf '\\nseveral shapes on one line\\n'

multi="curl -H \\"Authorization: Bearer gh""p_AAAABBBBCCCCDDDDEEEEFFFFGGGG\\" -d \\"slack=xox""b-1234567890-abcdefghijklmno\\""
printf '%s\\n' "$multi" > "$TMP/multi.txt"
out="$("$SCAN" "$TMP/multi.txt" 2>&1)"
n="$(printf '%s' "$out" | grep -c '^\\[')"
[ "$n" -eq 1 ] && pass "one line yields one finding" || fail "expected 1 finding for one line, got $n"
if printf '%s' "$out" | grep -q 'GitHub token' && printf '%s' "$out" | grep -q 'Slack token'; then
  pass "that finding names every shape on the line"
else
  fail "a shape on the line went unlabelled: $(printf '%s' "$out" | grep '^\\[')"
fi

printf '\\nmultiple files\\n'

printf 'nothing here\\n' > "$TMP/clean.txt"
printf 'DB_PASSWORD=Tr0ub4dor3notaplaceholder\\n' > "$TMP/dirty.txt"
"$SCAN" "$TMP/clean.txt" "$TMP/dirty.txt" >/dev/null 2>&1; rc=$?

Widening it trades directly against the floor that keeps prose from matching — a design call, documented in the PR rather than silently patched.

It also **withdrew M8** on its own analysis, reaching my conclusion by a better route than mine: the SLOT prohibition is conditioned on two tiers covering the same event, and orchard has one, so the example never triggers the rule. Its residual point was sharp enough to act on — my test asked what *exists* while the interview only sees what's *declared* — so the template now asks whether anything else recorded the same event, whether or not it was declared as a tier.

**1. It conflicts.** GitHub reports `CONFLICTING` / `DIRTY`. A test merge fails on four files. The sharpest is an **add/add conflict** on `knowledge/decisions/processing preserves the raw and synthesizes for a human, and extracts from the raw.md` — your own `/process` run (`2569249`) wrote a decision note at the identical path. There are two independently authored versions of the same note to reconcile, not a mechanical conflict.

**2. It implements an instruction the spec has since reversed.** Spec v1.3 (`889b68a`) explicitly reverses §6's `raw: unavailable` on the founding source note. My branch still carries it, with a stated reason — "transcript pruned" — that is false.

**3. It repeats a retracted claim, including in a place that ships.** `deltas.md:276` is the `preserve-stage` rationale: *"that graph's founding source note already points at a transcript that no longer exists. The material a source note exists to keep was destroyed…"* That rationale is what a future `/graph-patch` reads to adapt the edit into diverged prose in other graphs — so a false rationale there propagates outward. Same claim also live at `.claude/skills/process/SKILL.md:135-136`, the decision note line 29, and `changelog.md:14`.

**4. Still a draft with 0 reviews and 0 comments.**

**The error is mine, and it's the same one your commit `252564f` diagnoses.** On the 28th I ran `ls ~/.claude/projects/*claude-code-plugins*/284b79f5….jsonl`, got no matches, and reported to you: "Verified independently: 284b79f5 is genuinely absent." That glob could never have matched it — the session ran in a `commons-scaffold` worktree, so it sat under a *commons* slug. I searched the one path it couldn't be in and reported absence, while telling you I wasn't taking the note's word for it. The transcript is 2.8 MB and 1,311 lines. Searching by UUID across all slugs finds it in one command.

Worth noting the shape: I did apply the verify-first discipline, and it still produced a false confirmation, because I verified the claim against a query that presupposed the answer. That's what makes the failure durable enough that it got written into a decision note, a delta rationale, and the spec's closing argument before anyone caught it.

To make this mergeable I'd need to: merge `origin/main`, reconcile the two decision notes, drop `raw: unavailable`, and correct the retracted claim in the four places above — the delta rationale being the one that matters most, since it travels. The argument for the preserve stage survives, in the sharper form your commit message already found: the material wasn't destroyed, it was *undefended* — in no repository, backed up by nothing, its worktree deleted, invisible to the pipeline's own queue.

⏺ Clear resolution: keep my corrected structure, fold in main's cross-project glob fix — which also repairs the defective instruction I wrote into the orphaned path.

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/derek-personal/Developer/claude-code-plugins/.claude/worktrees/knowledge-commons-0.6.0-pipeline
python3 - <<'PY'
p = ".claude/skills/process/SKILL.md"
s = open(p).read()
start = s.index("<<<<<<< HEAD")
end = s.index(">>>>>>> origin/main") + len(">>>>>>> origin/main")
resolved = '''**A session is a conversation, not a project's property, and the glob above assumes otherwise.** A single
session can span several repositories — the founding one ran 590 turns in `commons` and 190 in
`claude-code-plugins`, across four working directories and three branches. Its transcript gets exactly one
home regardless, and which one is close to arbitrary. That one landed under a `commons` worktree slug,
where the `*claude-code-plugins*` pattern could never see it, and the graph concluded from the empty result
that the transcript had been pruned — a claim that reached this skill, a decision note, and a spec before
anyone checked. It was intact the whole time; it has since been moved here and archived at
`~/Developer/session-archive/`.

So: **before concluding any transcript is gone, search `~/.claude/projects/*/` by UUID**, not by project
glob. An empty result from the narrow pattern is evidence about the pattern. When a cross-project session
turns out to live elsewhere, moving it under the slug of the project that was its primary subject makes it
reachable — but that is a repair, not a rule, and the next such session will land wherever it lands.

**The chronicle is not a source tier.** A chronicle entry is the session-level synthesis — this
pipeline's own output, written for a human reader — so it lives in `types.synthesis`, not in `sources:`.
It used to be registered as a second, lower-ranked source tier, with an ordering between the two and a
guard against double-counting the same session through both. All of that existed to contain one
inversion: a distillation sitting on the input side. There is one raw source for a session, and it is
the transcript.

**When the transcript is genuinely gone.** Transcripts do get pruned, and this is the real case the
fallback tier was invented for. Where a session's transcript no longer exists but its chronicle entry
does, the run continues from the chronicle: extraction reads it instead of the transcript, and the source
note records `raw: unavailable` with the reason and the date the absence was observed — so the graph shows
a degraded note rather than leaving a reader to wonder later why it is thin. This is a permanent path, and
it is the only route by which a pre-existing chronicle entry whose session is gone can be processed at all.

It fires **only when the transcript is verifiably absent**, and "verifiably" carries the whole weight —
a UUID search across `~/.claude/projects/*/`, per the paragraph above, not a project-scoped glob that came
back empty. That distinction is not hypothetical: this graph marked its founding source note
`raw: unavailable` on the strength of a scoped miss, and the transcript was 2.8 MB and intact. Never fire
this path because a transcript is merely large, or because the chronicle is an easier read. Extracting
from the chronicle while the transcript is alive is exactly the failure step 4 exists to prevent, and this
path must not become the loophole for it. Where neither the transcript nor a chronicle entry exists, there
is nothing to process: say so and stop.'''
s = s[:start] + resolved + s[end:]
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("resolved: .claude/skills/process/SKILL.md")
PY

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/derek-personal/Developer/claude-code-plugins/.claude/worktrees/knowledge-commons-0.6.0-pipeline
python3 - <<'PY'
p = "plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh"
s = open(p).read()

old_block = """# Quote characters are deliberately NOT in this list. They were, and it inverted the
# gate: the value extracted from KEY="realsecret" begins with a quote, matched the
# placeholder test, and was dropped — so quoting a credential made it invisible, which
# is how most secrets are actually written in .env, YAML, JSON, and source. Quotes are
# stripped from the value before this test instead.
#
# Both halves are load-bearing on purpose and either one alone prevents the defect, so
# removing the "redundant" one looks safe and passes the suite. Keep both.

readonly PLACEHOLDER='([<{$[:space:]]|x{3,}|X{3,}|\\*{3,}|\\.\\.\\.|your[-_]|my[-_]|some[-_]|placeholder|example|changeme|change[-_]me|redacted|dummy|fake|sample|insert[-_]|todo|fixme|none|null|nil|true|false)'"""

new_block = """# Quote characters are deliberately NOT in this list. They were, and it inverted the
# gate: the value extracted from KEY="realsecret" begins with a quote, matched the
# placeholder test, and was dropped — so quoting a credential made it invisible, which
# is how most secrets are actually written in .env, YAML, JSON, and source. Quotes are
# stripped from the value before this test instead.
#
# Both halves are load-bearing on purpose and either one alone prevents the defect, so
# removing the "redundant" one looks safe and passes the suite. Keep both.
#
# TWO GROUPS, matched differently, and the difference is the whole point.
#
#   LEAD  — structural markers. A value that *begins* with one is a placeholder however
#           it continues: <your-key-here>, ${VAR}, xxxx, ***, ….
#   WORD  — dictionary words. These must be the WHOLE value, or be followed by a
#           non-alphanumeric character. Matching them as a prefix is a hole: a real
#           credential that happens to start with one is silently suppressed, and
#           "starts with a common English word" is not rare in a random key.
#           API_KEY=nullXk29LpQr7Wm3Zt8Vc1Bh6Ny4 read as clean under the prefix form,
#           as did values beginning true…, nil…, fake…, none…. Same shape as the two
#           defects already fixed here: a legitimate value defeating the heuristic
#           because the check was looser than the thing it was checking for.

readonly PLACEHOLDER_LEAD='[<{$[:space:]]|x{3,}|X{3,}|\\*{3,}|\\.\\.\\.'
readonly PLACEHOLDER_WORD='your|my|some|insert|placeholder|example|changeme|change|redacted|dummy|fake|sample|todo|fixme|none|null|nil|true|false'"""
assert s.count(old_block) == 1, "placeholder block"
s = s.replace(old_block, new_block)

old = """is_placeholder() {
  printf '%s' "$1" | grep -q -a -i -E -e "^$PLACEHOLDER"
}"""
new = """is_placeholder() {
  printf '%s' "$1" | grep -q -a -i -E -e "^($PLACEHOLDER_LEAD)" && return 0
  # Whole value, or the word followed by a separator (changeme, change-me, your-key…) —
  # never merely a prefix of a longer alphanumeric run.
  printf '%s' "$1" | grep -q -a -i -E -e "^($PLACEHOLDER_WORD)([^A-Za-z0-9]|\$)"
}"""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "is_placeholder"
s = s.replace(old, new)

# Drop the unused per-pattern flags machinery: no pattern sets it, and a dead branch in
# a security gate reads as a capability someone may rely on.
s = s.replace("""LABELS=()
FLAGS=()
REGEXES=()

add_pattern() { LABELS+=("$1"); FLAGS+=("$2"); REGEXES+=("$3"); }""",
"""LABELS=()
REGEXES=()

# Fixed-format patterns only; all are case-sensitive by construction (a token prefix
# has a fixed case). The two value-checked patterns below need -i and are scanned
# separately, so there is no per-pattern flag here to get out of step with reality.
add_pattern() { LABELS+=("$1"); REGEXES+=("$2"); }""")
import re
s = re.sub(r'add_pattern (".*?")\s+""\s+', lambda m: f'add_pattern {m.group(1)} ', s)
s = s.replace("""    label="${LABELS[$i]}"
    flags="${FLAGS[$i]}"
    re="${REGEXES[$i]}"
    i=$((i + 1))

    if [ "$flags" = "i" ]; then
      out="$(grep -a -n -i -E -e "$re" -- "$file")"
    else
      out="$(grep -a -n -E -e "$re" -- "$file")"
    fi
    rc=$?""",
"""    label="${LABELS[$i]}"
    re="${REGEXES[$i]}"
    i=$((i + 1))

    out="$(grep -a -n -E -e "$re" -- "$file")"
    rc=$?""")
s = s.replace('local file="$1" i label re flags out rc line lineno text value',
              'local file="$1" i label re out rc line lineno text value')
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("placeholder split + dead flags removed")
PY
bash -n plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh && echo "syntax ok"
grep -n 'add_pattern "private key\|add_pattern "JWT' plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh)
  ⎿  
  placeholder split + dead flags removed
  syntax ok

  107:add_pattern "JWT" 'eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}'

fix(knowledge-commons): a real secret starting with a placeholder word was suppressed

Found by the claude-review check on PR #71. Fourth instance of the same class,
and the third found by someone other than me.

is_placeholder tested the extracted value with a case-insensitive PREFIX match,
and PLACEHOLDER carried bare dictionary words. So a credential that merely began
with one was silently dropped:

  API_KEY=nullXk29LpQr7Wm3Zt8Vc1Bh6Ny4   -> exit 0, clean
  PASSWORD=trueBl00dSecretValue99        -> exit 0, clean
  API_KEY=fakeR3alSecretValue123456      -> exit 0, clean
  SECRET=nilsHiddenPassphrase2026        -> exit 0, clean

28 characters, well past the 8-character floor, and reported clean. "Starts with
a common English word" is not rare in a random key. The review named null, none,
nil, true and false; the hole was wider — fake, sample, example, todo and the
rest behaved the same way.

The placeholder set is now two groups matched differently. Structural markers
(<, {, $, leading whitespace, xxx, ***, ...) still match as a prefix, because a
value that starts that way is a placeholder however it continues. Dictionary
words must be the whole value or be followed by a non-alphanumeric character —
so changeme, change-me and your-key-here are still suppressed while nullXk29…
is reported.

Also drops the per-pattern case-insensitivity flag. No pattern set it, and the
suite caught that: its fail-closed test injects a broken pattern using the old
three-argument add_pattern, which under the new signature passed an empty regex
rather than a malformed one, so the scan reported hits instead of failing. An
empty regex matches every line. Dead generality in a security gate is not free.

Six regression cases added, suite 35 -> 41, verified red on the prefix behaviour.
False-positive rate unchanged: 0 across the markdown corpus, 11 on the 4.7 MB
transcript.

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/derek-personal/Developer/claude-code-plugins/.claude/worktrees/knowledge-commons-0.6.0-pipeline
python3 - <<'PY'
p = "plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh"
s = open(p).read()

old_assign = """    while IFS= read -r line; do
      [ -z "$line" ] && continue
      lineno="${line%%:*}"
      text="${line#*:}"
      value="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$ASSIGN_RE" | head -1)"
      value="$(extract_value "$value")"
      [ -z "$value" ] && continue
      is_placeholder "$value" && continue
      # A value shorter than 8 characters is not a credential worth stopping a run over.
      [ "${#value}" -lt 8 ] && continue
      report "$ASSIGN_LABEL" "$file" "$lineno" "$text"
    done <<EOF
$out
EOF"""
new_assign = """    while IFS= read -r line; do
      [ -z "$line" ] && continue
      lineno="${line%%:*}"
      text="${line#*:}"
      # EVERY assignment on the line, not just the first. Checking only the first and
      # skipping the line on its verdict is fail-open: `DEBUG_SECRET=todo
      # PROD_PASSWORD=Tr0ub4dor3xyz` discarded the whole line because the leading match
      # was a placeholder, and reported clean with a real credential still in it.
      matches="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$ASSIGN_RE")"
      [ -z "$matches" ] && continue
      while IFS= read -r m; do
        [ -z "$m" ] && continue
        value="$(extract_value "$m")"
        [ -z "$value" ] && continue
        is_placeholder "$value" && continue
        # A value shorter than 8 characters is not a credential worth stopping a run over.
        [ "${#value}" -lt 8 ] && continue
        report "$ASSIGN_LABEL" "$file" "$lineno" "$text"
        break
      done <<INNER
$matches
INNER
    done <<EOF
$out
EOF"""
assert s.count(old_assign) == 1, "assign loop"
s = s.replace(old_assign, new_assign)

old_auth = """    while IFS= read -r line; do
      [ -z "$line" ] && continue
      lineno="${line%%:*}"
      text="${line#*:}"
      value="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$AUTH_RE" | head -1 \\
               | sed -E 's/.*(bearer|basic|token)[[:space:]]+//I; s/^["'"'"']//')"
      [ -z "$value" ] && continue
      is_placeholder "$value" && continue
      report "$AUTH_LABEL" "$file" "$lineno" "$text"
    done <<EOF
$out
EOF"""
new_auth = """    while IFS= read -r line; do
      [ -z "$line" ] && continue
      lineno="${line%%:*}"
      text="${line#*:}"
      # Same reasoning as the assignment scan above: every match on the line.
      matches="$(printf '%s' "$text" | grep -a -o -i -E -e "$AUTH_RE")"
      [ -z "$matches" ] && continue
      while IFS= read -r m; do
        [ -z "$m" ] && continue
        value="$(printf '%s' "$m" | sed -E 's/.*(bearer|basic|token)[[:space:]]+//I; s/^["'"'"']//')"
        [ -z "$value" ] && continue
        is_placeholder "$value" && continue
        report "$AUTH_LABEL" "$file" "$lineno" "$text"
        break
      done <<INNER
$matches
INNER
    done <<EOF
$out
EOF"""
assert s.count(old_auth) == 1, "auth loop"
s = s.replace(old_auth, new_auth)
s = s.replace('local file="$1" i label re out rc line lineno text value',
              'local file="$1" i label re out rc line lineno text value matches m')
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("both loops now check every match on a line")
PY

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/derek-personal/Developer/claude-code-plugins/.claude/worktrees/knowledge-commons-0.6.0-pipeline
python3 - <<'PY'
p = "plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh"
s = open(p).read()

old = """extract_value() {
  printf '%s' "$1" | sed -E 's/^[^:=]*[:=][[:space:]]*//; s/^["'"'"']//; s/["'"'"']$//'
}"""
new = """# Sets EXTRACTED rather than printing, and uses parameter expansion rather than sed.
# Both are for speed, and the speed is not cosmetic: this runs once per match, and a
# rendered transcript is one enormous JSON line after another. The printf|sed form cost
# three subshells per match and took nine minutes on a 4.5 MB transcript — long enough
# that a preserve stage would start looking hung.
EXTRACTED=""
extract_value() {
  local v="$1"
  v="${v#*[:=]}"
  while [ "$v" != "${v#[[:space:]]}" ]; do v="${v#[[:space:]]}"; done
  v="${v#[\\"\\']}"
  v="${v%[\\"\\']}"
  EXTRACTED="$v"
}"""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "extract_value"
s = s.replace(old, new)

old = """is_placeholder() {
  printf '%s' "$1" | grep -q -a -i -E -e "^($PLACEHOLDER_LEAD)" && return 0
  # Whole value, or the word followed by a separator (changeme, change-me, your-key…) —
  # never merely a prefix of a longer alphanumeric run.
  printf '%s' "$1" | grep -q -a -i -E -e "^($PLACEHOLDER_WORD)([^A-Za-z0-9]|\\$)"
}"""
new = """# Bash's own =~ instead of a grep subshell, for the same reason as extract_value.
# nocasematch is set once at the top; the case statements elsewhere compare fixed
# label and path strings, which case-insensitivity does not affect.
is_placeholder() {
  [[ "$1" =~ ^($PLACEHOLDER_LEAD) ]] && return 0
  # Whole value, or the word followed by a separator (changeme, change-me, your-key…) —
  # never merely a prefix of a longer alphanumeric run.
  [[ "$1" =~ ^($PLACEHOLDER_WORD)([^A-Za-z0-9]|$) ]] && return 0
  return 1
}"""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "is_placeholder"
s = s.replace(old, new)

s = s.replace("set -uo pipefail\n", "set -uo pipefail\nshopt -s nocasematch\n", 1)
s = s.replace("""        value="$(extract_value "$m")"
        [ -z "$value" ] && continue""",
"""        extract_value "$m"; value="$EXTRACTED"
        [ -z "$value" ] && continue""")
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("hot path moved to bash builtins")
PY
bash -n plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh && echo "syntax ok"
bash plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/test-scan-secrets.sh >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "suite exit=$? ($(bash plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/test-scan-secrets.sh | grep -cE '^  ok') cases)")
  ⎿  
  hot path moved to bash builtins
  syntax ok
  suite exit=0 (45 cases)

git commit -q -F - <<'MSG'
fix(knowledge-commons): check every credential on a line, and keep the scan fast

Found by the claude-review check on PR #71. Fifth instance of the class, and the
narrowest yet — but still a real secret reported clean.

Both value-checked scans took only the FIRST match on a line (grep -o | head -1)
and skipped the whole line on its verdict. So a placeholder or a short value
earlier on the line hid a real credential later on it:

  DEBUG_SECRET=todo PROD_PASSWORD=Tr0ub4dor3xyzLONGVALUE   -> exit 0, clean
  KEY=abc API_PASSWORD=R3alSecretValueHere123              -> exit 0, clean

The per-line multi-shape fix in 42182b4 did not cover this: it accumulates across
DIFFERENT patterns, while this is one pattern matching twice on a line. The suite's
"several shapes on one line" case used two different pattern types for exactly that
reason, so it could not have caught it. Both scans now check every match.

That fix cost nine minutes on a 4.5 MB transcript — a rendered transcript is one
enormous JSON line after another, and the naive form spawned three subshells per
match. extract_value now uses parameter expansion and sets a global instead of
printing into a command substitution, and is_placeholder uses bash's own =~ rather
than a grep subshell. 541s -> 4s on the same file, same 11 findings.

Suite 41 -> 45, verified red on both the head -1 behaviour and the placeholder
prefix behaviour after the rewrite.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

⏺ Bash(echo "sed: $(sed --version 2>/dev/null | head -1 || echo 'BSD/macOS (no --version)')"

  abcdefghijklmnop
    rc=0
  === and case-sensitively (what it does without I) ===
  Authorization: BEARER abcdefghijklmnop

⏺ `I` works on this macOS sed, so it isn't broken today — but the reviewer's underlying point holds: if that sed ever fails, `value` comes back empty and `[ -z "$value" ] && continue` silently drops the finding, which is a fail-open sitting next to meticulously fail-closed code. Fixing it with the builtin, and merging the newer main.

⏺ Bash(cd /Users/derek-personal/Developer/claude-code-plugins/.claude/worktrees/knowledge-commons-0.6.0-pipeline
python3 - <<'PY'
p = "plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/scan-secrets.sh"
s = open(p).read()
old = """        value="$(printf '%s' "$m" | sed -E 's/.*(bearer|basic|token)[[:space:]]+//I; s/^["'"'"']//')"
        [ -z "$value" ] && continue"""
new = """        # Bash's own =~ rather than sed's s///I. The I flag is a GNU extension; it
        # happens to work on the macOS sed this was written against, but the failure it
        # would cause elsewhere is the shape this file exists to avoid — sed errors, the
        # command substitution yields nothing, and the empty-value guard below drops a
        # real finding with no error and no non-zero exit. nocasematch is already set.
        value=""
        [[ "$m" =~ (bearer|basic|token)[[:space:]]+(.*) ]] && value="${BASH_REMATCH[2]}"
        value="${value#[\\"\\']}"
        [ -z "$value" ] && continue"""
assert s.count(old) == 1, "auth extraction"
s = s.replace(old, new)
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("sed -I dependency removed from the Authorization path")
PY

  printf '%s\n' "$v" > "$T/a.txt"; "$S" "$T/a.txt" >/dev/null 2>&1; printf "  exit=%s  %s\n" "$?" "$v"
done
echo "  (last must be 0; others 1)"
bash plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/test-scan-secrets.sh >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "suite exit=$? ($(bash plugins/knowledge-commons/scripts/test-scan-secrets.sh | grep -cE '^  ok') cases)")
